# exagium: one queue after the whole-project audit (2026-11-17)

Built from six skill-group audits (A security, B tests/bugs/state, C structure, D claims, E agents/free compute, F ML/binaries), a separate verification pass (S2: 15 of 16 high-impact items confirmed by reproduction, 1 partly right), and the 2026-11-13 audit and handoffs. Severity is after S2 corrections. Source of truth: queue.json; render with render.py. Every open item declares what it writes ('touches': files, paths, branches; reads don't count); render.py refuses an open item without it and lists items in different batches that touch the same thing, so overlaps show up as items are added.

| P11 | B9 | 6 | none | high | build | Knowledge store: one queryable schema for parts, tasks, runs, per-item outcomes, spec cards and sources | Owner, 2026-11-19: add to the queue only, no work yet. Hypothesis: compartments, hidden connections, composition, tool-making and void-finding all need every result in one place with per-item detail; today results are JSON files across loop/results, runs/, bench and research. Smallest version: one schema (SQLite or JSONL plus an index), filled by a script from existing results, never hand-edited; ledger stays the source of truth and the store is rebuilt from it. Ingest P3's per-item output when it lands. First test: can one query answer 'which family wins on which items of which task, at what cost' for optdigits plus P1's tasks? Debt check for the auditor: count of result files the store does not ingest. Owner 2026-11-19 (adds to what exists; does not change how anything works): keep per-item detail and provenance, never only averages (two parts at 0.97 can fail on different items: that difference is the signal); record edges between parts as well as parts: derived-from (lineage, as in AlphaEvolve's archive), combined-with, distilled-into; every stored measurement is a pinned frame (hash, task, host, split, uncertainty) and the motion between frames (what changed, up or down) is queryable; recompute, don't relabel, as the collection grows. Whole-system review, 2026-11-19 (owner asked to look past current work; adds, does not change how anything works): also store every plan's forecast (P23) and score calibration per source (Claude, each worker, the owner); provenance edges claim -> run -> code digest -> data; the web view (P21) reads from the store once it exists. | P20e | B3 | 5 | minor | high | build | Spread work across the whole free workforce: parallel lanes and routing each job to the equivalent pool with most room (capacity planning across pools and hours) | GATE MET 2026-11-19 (owner: maximise output across the free agents and compute without hurting ability or rules). Measured that day: NVIDIA 37 calls (~0.3% of the measured refill), OpenRouter 0 of 1,000/day for the same Nemotron model, Groq 24 of 1,000, Cloudflare ~20% of neurons, Gemini 0, GitHub Actions almost always one job at a time of 20 allowed; the worker and P5 both stopped on the NVIDIA cap while the other pools sat idle. Done now (no test confounded): P5 scores proposals in parallel, one job each (b6b1279; dry run 44512560102 verified). Design (hypothesis): (1) lanes: up to k worker runs at once (the queue's CAS already allows overlap) and parallel proposal batches, k set from budget headroom and ready items; (2) routing: each job type has a list of equivalent pools (Nemotron on NIM or OpenRouter; gpt-oss-120b on Groq or Cloudflare), the ledger picks the one with most room; (3) other free models (Gemini Flash-Lite, Cloudflare's models) as labelled arms, which also gives P20a per-model data. TEST, rule fixed now: 2 days as now against 2 days with lanes and routing (interleaved if possible); adopt only if items finished plus valid proposals per day at least double AND worker acceptance and P5 valid rate are not lower (Wilson intervals overlap or better). Waits for P5's first 20 (Nemotron on NIM only, as pre-registered) and Q58's 48 h unattended test, so neither result is confounded. 2026-11-20 (owner: don't overwhelm any one agent or model): reviews now spread by headroom: router/pick.py picks qualified reviewers from the least-used pools in the shared daily ledger (engines=auto is review.yml's default; explicit engines are dropped if their pool is full), NIM and Groq added to review.yml and routed through compat_call, and review calls are recorded in the same ledger the worker reads (main 1990fcf5; smoke run 44668157533 picked Groq and NIM and both calls landed in ledger/2026-11-20.json). Cause: on 2026-11-20 unledgered reviews and probes took ~7,600 of Cloudflare's 10,000 free daily neurons; the worker saw 2,406 and stalled. Still unledgered: decisions-probe, confirm and P5's own calls outside the worker. Own slip: c6559316 fixed a test that the previous commit pushed failing (commit chained on tail again). | queue P20; project doc claude/forge-free-pools.md |

| P7f | B7 | 2 | minor | high | build | Research depth: the scan finds, a drill restructures full text into a queryable store, models query only what fits their context | Cards keep 1-3 verified claims because the checker needs a verbatim quote with numbers and the reader sees only abstracts, which rarely carry measurements. Steps, each kept only if its test shows more verified claims per card at an equal or better verification rate: (1) full text of the top papers (arXiv HTML, open-access PDFs from OpenAlex's oa_url), the reader seeing the results sections within the model's measured context (P20a); (2) one round of citation following (OpenAlex referenced_works and cited_by) and refined queries from first-pass results; (3) per-source cards (hit rate, errors, verified claims per call) from the logs, used to order and skip sources per question kind, the same engine as P20's worker cards. Also missing: general web search (no reliable keyless option from CI; candidates need an owner key and a check of current free tiers). Related: P7e (source discovery and per-source credibility from track record) covers finding niche communities; step 3 here (per-source cards by measured yield) shares its data, so P7f's step 3 is done there if P7e lands first. Owner 2026-11-19 21:41: 'depth injection': search is the scan; a drill grows through the content and restructures it into a queryable form that works with context limits, not against them. Design (hypothesis): (1) the drill takes the scan's top sources with full text (arXiv HTML, open-access PDFs via OpenAlex oa_url); (2) restructures each into a store: sections, every results-table row, and claims with numbers and conditions, each with exact character offsets in the source (typed triples with provenance, the P11/P21 format); (3) readers query the store (filters or SQL) and get only matching rows, sized to the reading model's measured context (P20a), instead of reading documents; every returned row is checked against the text by script; (4) a claim missing its method or baseline triggers a drill into the cited paper, within a fixed per-card budget. TEST, rule fixed now: rebuild the R1-R8 cards drilled vs abstract-only (same questions, same reading models); adopt if verified claims of grade 'measured' per card at least double, the script verification rate is not lower, and free calls per verified claim do not rise by more than half. | queue P7, P20a; research/cards/R1-R8 (1-3 claims each) |

| P18 | B1 |  | none | high | test | Jail assurance: prove isolation holds, refused things stay refused, allowed things still work, and the search cannot game its own checker | Owner, 2026-11-19: add to the queue only, no work yet. Purpose is defensive: the owner's own sandbox, in the owner's own repo. Owner's point: attackers game the system as it is, so known-wall tests are not enough. Layers: (1) conformance suite: network, files outside the work folder, secrets and env, child processes, resource limits, forbidden syscalls must be refused; legitimate work must pass; each test proven to bite by switching its restriction off (mutation check). (2) Defence in depth and least privilege: nothing worth taking inside the jail (key never in the same job), so one breached wall is not enough. (3) Fuzz the boundary with existing tools (syscall and argument fuzzing inside the jail) rather than hand-written exploits; known weaknesses checked by version and setting against published advisories with existing scanners. (4) The realistic creative adversary here is the search itself: evolved candidates will game meters and checkers (reward hacking); log and test for it as a standing threat (frozen evaluator, held-out checks, anomaly flags on implausible gains). (5) Assume breach: detect and limit blast radius (job isolation, short-lived tokens, audit of what each job touched). Covers runner/jail.py and loop/confine.c (loop.py frozen: tests only, no edits). Whole-system review, 2026-11-19 (owner asked to look past current work; adds, does not change how anything works): at scale add contributor gaming: fake accounts, leaderboard gaming, and an independent reproducer assigned to any result before it gains authority. | runner/jail.py, loop/confine.c, loop/seccomp_filter.h on main; runner/results/2026-11-18-q10-q55/ (Q10 jail runs); skills: careful-live-changes, security-code-review, adversarial-review |

DECISIVE TEST FIRST (P20a's data): on the release tests' graded outcomes and the call logs, does routing by measured cards beat fixed assignment on cost per accepted item at equal or better acceptance (replayed offline)? Design (hypothesis): git as the coordinator: each item gets claimed_by and lease_until; a worker claims by committing to queue.json, and git rejects a second concurrent push, so no double-ups; an expired lease frees the item. A scheduled Actions job picks the top ready item whose tier allows a free model, claims it, does it (key and generated code in separate jobs, as P5), writes the result, a run note and the new status, and releases. Trust tiers from data (bench-2 Nemotron 3/3, bench-5 NIM 11/15: good, not unchecked): docs/mechanical (B5, B4) may self-merge after a script confirms only targeted lines changed and a second free model reviews; code goes to a PR with tests and free review, final check by Claude or the owner; design stays with Claude. A daily digest drafts the handoff and README update, flagging any number without an openable artifact. P5 (proposer) becomes one job type of this worker. Cheapest test: B5 docs items, two workers at once to force claim races; measure double claims (target 0), share of free drafts accepted on spot check, stuck leases. Limits and pacing (owner, 2026-11-18): a budget ledger per pool and period (minute, day, month, $), seeded from measured limits: Actions free and unlimited on this public repo, 20 concurrent jobs (GitHub docs); OpenRouter 1,000 req/day; Groq 1,000 req/day and 8,000 tokens/min (headers, run 44197136667); Cloudflare 10k neurons/day; NIM shape unmeasured (about 30/min for 10 min, then 3-5/min refusals, bench-5); Cerebras/xAI/Gemini in $ via P4. A run reserves budget by commit before calling (same CAS as claims); live remaining-quota headers override the seed; a 429 marks the pool exhausted until its window resets; the daily budget is spread across runs with ~10% kept for probes and reviews; unknown shapes (NIM) become low-priority measurement jobs; each job takes the cheapest pool measured good enough for its kind of work with budget left, else CPU-only work or a clean stop with the reason recorded. Builds on P4 (spend caps) and Q22 (usage log). Coexisting with chat sessions (owner, 2026-11-18): the worker never calls Claude, so chats never draw on its budget and it never draws on the owner's Claude limit. Shared resources get rules: chats claim items like workers (claimed_by chat-<session>) and a chat claim wins; the worker skips any batch that touches the same paths; each free pool keeps a reserved share (start at 20%) for chat-started reviews; the worker caps itself at about half of Actions' 20 concurrent jobs; it commits only to its own branches/PRs (plus claim commits); a repo variable the owner can flip pauses new claims. Block s has its own rule W[s], a unit-norm vector drawn with np.random.default_rng(seed); x ~ N(0, 1) per column; y = 1 if x . W[s] > 0 else 0. X is x quantized to bytes: clip(round((x + 4) / 8 * 255), 0, 255) as uint8; y is uint8. Add tasks/test_drift.py (unittest): the same seed gives identical X and y bytes; another seed differs; with n=4000 each block's share of 1s is between 0.4 and 0.6; the sign rule from W[0] agrees with y on block 0 above 0.95 and on the last block below 0.8 (so drift is present). | Q59 | B3 |  | minor | medium | fix | Worker acceptance misses in release test 2: an accurate line edited (D21), a direct commit where a PR was required (DB), a PR that weakened a guarantee (Q38), an empty card landed as a PR (R4) | Per test 2's outcome rule each failure becomes an item. Hypotheses to test, one each, against the 30 graded items as a replay set: (1) a 'check only' instruction (D21) should allow no edit unless the checker finds an error; (2) agent-facing paths must route to a PR before commit (DB was committed directly; find why the path rule missed tests/canary-BRIEF.md); (3) the reviewer should reject a change that weakens a guarantee's strength (Q38), not only redundancy and vagueness; (4) a card with zero surviving claims should end as 'no change' with a note, not a PR (R4). Fix rule: each change must flip its failure on replay without flipping any of the 26 acceptable items.
